- **Closed: P1-start c, the "Final" bug (D40 sweep).** Plan `docs/plans/P1-start.md`. `fetch_series_status` counted a postponed or cancelled game in a series standing and `games_today[].status` of the snapshot said "Final" for it; both now read the pair through `schema.game_status` (the D40 table). The series schedule schema now requires `detailedState` (the source always sends it; a series entry without it is a recorded `mlb_series_status` / `malformed_response`, never a guess). An earlier game of the series counts if the table says `final`; it is skipped if `upcoming`, `live`, Postponed or Cancelled; any other pair (Suspended, "Completed Early: Rain", a new one) is unknown, so the whole standing is refused as `malformed_response` (the reviewer's point: skipping it would show an earlier game's standing as current). ### 2026-10-20 - P0.17-c: the git guard covers PowerShell and wrapped git (QA6-02)

- **Scope:** `.claude/settings.json` has a `PowerShell` matcher running the same script as `Bash` (checked live in this session: `powershell -Command "git status --short"` through the PowerShell tool was blocked by the hook). `git_guard.py` now also: blocks git inside `bash/sh/zsh -c`, `eval`, `powershell`/`pwsh -Command` (and `-EncodedCommand` always), `cmd /c`, `python -c`, `node -e` (also perl, ruby, php, deno, bun), `Invoke-Expression`/`iex`, `Invoke-Command`, `Start-Process`, `ForEach-Object`, `xargs`, and a command word built at run time next to git (`x=git; $x push`); reads git through `env`, `command`, `&`, `{ }`, `( )`, `$out =`, `$(...)` and backticks (quoted or not) and through a program path (`C:\Program Files\Git\cmd\git.exe`); blocks `filter-branch`/`filter-repo`, `git branch -d/-D/-m` of `v2` (main was already blocked), `git remote add/set-url/remove/rm/rename/set-head/set-branches/prune`; allows reading `core.hooksPath` (`git config core.hooksPath`, `--get`, `--get-all`, with `--local/--global`) and blocks writing it (a value, `--unset`, `--add`, `--replace-all`, `--edit`). PowerShell here-strings (`@'...'@`) are skipped like heredocs.
- **Red before the fix:** with the old guard and the new tests, 59 failed, 166 passed (the passing ones are the allow-controls). After round 1 of the review: 267 passed in `tests/test_agent_git_guard.py` (was 120; 225 before the round): every command of the QA list, the same class (other spellings, passed along, substitutions), 18 new git rules, 41 allow-controls (the documented reads, wrappers that do not mention git, scripts and modules with "git" in their name, PowerShell and shell habits around plain git), the wiring of both matchers, and the script run with a PowerShell event.
- **Decision inside the plan:** wrapper strings that mention git are blocked even if the inner command would be harmless (`bash -c "git status"`): the session runs git directly, where the hook can read it. A `python -m pytest tests/test_agent_git_guard.py` or `bash script.sh` is not a wrapper string and passes.
- **Rulesets checked with `gh api` (2026-10-20):** `main` (id 25520388: deletion, non_fast_forward, update), `v2` (25520406: deletion, non_fast_forward), `tags` (25520422: `refs/tags/v1.0.0-kaggle`, deletion and update), all `active`, and `rules/branches/main` lists the three rules. **All three have no bypass actor.** Consequence for the owner: the `update` rule on `main` also stops the owner's release merge of `v2` and the daily bot commit of D15 (`public/data/`, `evals/live/`) until a bypass actor (the owner, and the Actions app for the bot) is added. Nothing was changed on GitHub by this session.
- **Reviewer round 1 (BLOCK, fixed):** (1) `git branch -m main` passed because `-m` is read as a message option by the ref scanner: the branch rule now checks `main` and `master` with `v2`; (2) AGENTS.md now says `gh` and the REST API are not read by the hook and the rulesets are only as strong as the token's admin rights (option (a); blocking mutating `gh api` calls was not done); (3) combined and abbreviated options bypassed the force and no-verify checks (`git push -fu`, `--forc`, `--no-ver`, `--amen`, `--har`, `--mirro`, `clean -fd`): pre-existing, but fixed as a class (an unambiguous abbreviation of a dangerous long option is read as the option; a short cluster containing `f` is a force, one containing `d` on a push is a delete). Also from the optionals: `GIT_CONFIG_*` carrying `core.hooksPath` in the command's environment, `wsl`/`ssh`/`find -exec` with git. Red: 19 of the 42 new tests failed on the previous guard, 23 are named controls (options that look dangerous and are not). 267 passed in the file after round 1.
- **Reviewer round 2 (BLOCK; D35 stop: two rounds without PASS):** the ref scanner skipped the argument of `-m/-c/-C/-F` for every subcommand, so `git switch -c main`, `switch -C main`, `checkout -m main`, `reset -m main` passed; and `git pull -r`, `--rebase=true` passed the rebase rule. Fixed after the round and NOT reviewed again: the skip applies only to commit, merge, revert, cherry-pick, am, tag, stash and notes (`MESSAGE_SUBCOMMANDS`); `pull -r`/`--rebase=<anything but false>` is a rebase. 13 of 25 new tests failed on the previous guard, 12 are named controls (`git commit -m main`, `merge -m main v2`, `switch -c experiment/spike`, `pull --rebase=false`). 292 passed in the file. Not fixed (the reviewer's optionals): `git fetch -f` and `git add -f` pass while `--force` is blocked, `git config --edit/--unset user.name`.
- **Reviewer round 3 (owner-authorized third round): PASS.** No finding blocks; the only change from round 2 in about 400 ordinary commands is `git branch -c main copy`, now blocked (harmless, fails safe). Owner's decisions, 2026-10-21: `gh` and the REST API not covered by the hook goes to P1.0; the `main` ruleset has no bypass actor and the owner will disable it temporarily for the release merge; the guard gaps listed here stay as recorded.
- **Known false positives (fail safe):** a backtick inside a single-quoted commit message is read as a command substitution; a PowerShell backtick line continuation after `git push`; `Where-Object { $_.Name -like '*git*' }` counts as a wrapper that mentions git. Not fixed: `git update-ref -d refs/heads/v2` and `git config remote.origin.url <x>` (local only, the rulesets are the backstop), `git\ push` and a program name built from pieces without the word git.
- **Not covered, written in AGENTS.md:** other tools, scripts that run git by themselves, shell functions and aliases; the rulesets and the `commit-msg` hook are the lines for those.

- **Scope:** `PITCH_VALUE_BOUNDS` (one table, physical sanity limits, not thresholds) turns a populated Statcast cell outside 30 < `release_speed` <= 110, |`pfx_x`|, |`pfx_z`| <= 3, |`plate_x`| <= 5, -3 <= `plate_z` <= 10 into `malformed_rows` for its group (the containment of P0.16-b, renamed `_bounded_pitch_measurements`). Means and medians go through `_aggregate`/`_finite_or_none`: an aggregate that is not finite is unavailable, a movement with one unreadable component has no pair. `mlb_schemas` gets the kinds `count` (>= 0), `positive` (>= 1, `seriesGameNumber`, added after reviewer round 1) and `text`; ERA, WHIP, innings, OPS and average are `text` and the fetcher keeps only numbers (`_season_figure`, `_rate_stat`), so a placeholder is that figure unavailable ("N/A" or `null`), not a recorded error and not a stop. Contract: rule 2 paragraphs "within bounds" and "MLB API counts and placeholders".
- **Red before the fix:** with `data_pipeline/` stashed, `tests/test_domain_bounds.py` + `tests/test_source_schemas.py` gave 89 failed, 200 passed, 7 skipped (the count tests are generated from the schemas and skip when the schema has no `count` kind, so a guard test fails instead). After: 355 passed in the two files; full suite 3353 passed (+180), 2 skipped.

Placeholders record nothing: they are what the API legitimately says for a debut. Team OPS/average got the same treatment (class sweep; that API path is not used by the live pipeline, which derives team splits from Statcast).

Mutations: the badge back to the UTC date (1 red), the New York zone replaced by Los Angeles (2 red). Winter time is covered in the function and in the badge (`2026-01-05T04:30Z` is the 4th).
- **Hydration note:** `GameCarousel` is a client component that the server also renders; the two clocks differ only in the seconds around midnight in New York, when the label may flip between the server's render and the browser's. Left as it is (a page is a snapshot of a file; the label is one word); not worth a `suppressHydrationWarning` that would hide real mismatches.
- **Real-page check (Playwright tools not used: the page is server-rendered, so `curl` of `next start` shows what the server says):** a live payload dated today in New York (2026-10-16, when the UTC date was the same) shows "Today"; the effect at midnight UTC is what the fake-clock tests fix at 01:30 UTC. The served file was restored.
- **Sweep:** every `toISOString()`/`new Date()` in `app/`, `components/` and `lib/`: the other `new Date(...)` (`formatStartTime`, `newYorkDay`) only parse an instant the file gives; `todayInNewYork` is now the only reader of the machine's clock, and `DateBadge` was the only one that used it.

### 2026-10-16 — P0.15-D2.4d each workflow step says what it did (F19; same schema 0.7.0) (Claude Code, D35 lane)

- **Class:** a status written fixed. One mechanism: `_run_agents` returns an `AgentRun` that records, per agent, the matchups it finished; `_build_agent_workflow(matchup_ids, run)` writes `completed` only for a step every counted matchup went through, else `pending` (`_step_status`); the postgame step is as before (from the starters graded). The names of the five agents are one constant (`schema.WORKFLOW_AGENTS`), read by the generator and the manifest.
- **The manifest recomputes it from the payload** (`provenance.step_status`, `STEP_EVIDENCE`): a step is `completed` exactly when every matchup of the file carries what its agent leaves there: `analyst.*` claims (analyst), `writer.*` claims and sections (writer), a `reviewed` critic review with checks and `critic.*` claims (critic); otherwise `pending`. For these three two sources agree on a published file (the run's record and the payload). **Limit (reviewer round 1, BLOCK, accepted):** the scout leaves no trace of its own in the file; its evidence is only that the confidence packet is there (the schema requires it and the live matchup builder writes it), so the manifest cannot refute its `completed`, and only the run record backs it. Written in the contract (6.2), in the code comment of `STEP_EVIDENCE` and in a test that names it; a hand-written "completed" with the work missing, or a "pending" with all of it, is `rule1:derivation_mismatch#agent_workflow.steps[].status`. ### 2026-10-16 — P0.15-D2.3c errors, warnings and reasons are `{source, code}` (F10; same schema 0.6.0, D38) (Claude Code, D35 lane)

- **Context:** the previous session was cut by the usage limit before it read the reviewer's verdict on b. This session found b complete in the index, re-ran the verification, ran the reviewer (round 1 BLOCK: `StarterCard.tsx` defaulted a switch pitcher to "RHP"; fixed with a shared `throwingHand` and a component test; round 2 PASS) and committed b as `72a2a64` (CI green) before starting c. - **Class:** the public file said what the internals said. One mechanism: `live_pipeline._record_error(source, code, message, **context)` (the code is mandatory), `_record_failure(source, exc, ...)` for a caught exception, and `error_code(exc)` as the only place that maps one: new classes `MalformedResponse`, `TruncatedResponse`, `MissingColumn`, `MalformedRows`, `UnusableColumn`, `UnknownValue` at the raise sites (the three that were `ValueError`/`RuntimeError` keep their base class, so no caller changes), `OSError` is `request_failed`, anything else `unexpected_error`. `generate_demo_json._run_summary` publishes `errors` and `warnings` through `_published` (exactly `{source, code}`, deduplicated, at most 20; an unknown source or code becomes `unexpected_error`, never the text); `reason` is `null` for a live file and `{source, code}` for a fallback (list in the contract, rule 8). ### 2026-10-15 — P0.15-D2.2f figures that cannot be true together and an incomplete Statcast sample (F21) (Claude Code, D35 lane)

- **Owner's instructions:** commit f of `docs/plans/P0.15-D2.2.md` under D35 (no yes per commit; the reviewer and the stops of D35); first, check the hook is active (`git push --dry-run origin main` is blocked with its reason before git: it was); calibration with the API (at most 30 cached requests, stop if it needs more than 1 per appearance); `live_unknown_figures` and `live_limited_arsenal` must come from the real path of F21; at the close of D2.2 the summary in STATUS and the plan of D2.3 in plan mode, then stop.
- **Calibration (real data):** the cached Statcast frames (43 pitchers, 42,931 regular-season and postseason rows after de-duplicating by pitch) against the API line of 30 pitchers, one request each (`stats=byDateRange`, 2026-01-01 through the pitcher's last regular-season game in the frame), cached under `data_cache/raw/mlb_api_calibration`. 28 pitchers are comparable (818 appearances, 9,414 batters faced; relievers with up to 81 appearances): the gap is 0 in every one, intentional walks included. The two others, traded in-season, have a cached frame of their second club only (12 of 15 appearances, 11 of 66): incomplete against their aggregate season line, but the pipeline reads `splits[0]` (the first club), so F21 catches one (670167: 43 against 203) and misses the other (605242: 46 against 15) until F22 reads the aggregate. The reviewer found that my first wording ("the check calls both incomplete") was true only of the aggregate line; it is corrected here and in contract 6.5. The fixture `tests/fixtures/statcast_vs_api_pairs.json` keeps the pairs. My first union of the cached files double-counted rows that two overlapping queries had both fetched; de-duplicating by `(pitcher, game_pk, at_bat_number, pitch_number)` removed it (it had shown gaps of -296 and -260 that were mine, not the data's).
- **Tolerance:** the plan said one plate appearance per appearance (the API credits the batter to the pitcher who started the plate appearance, Statcast to the one who finished it). Measured: 0. I set `STATCAST_PA_TOLERANCE = 0` instead of the number of games because one per appearance makes the check vacuous for any pitcher with 25 or more appearances (a whole missing start passes) and the data shows the mechanism does not happen. It is a named constant and the contract (6.5) says how to change it. This is a tightening of the plan's number, inside what the calibration was for; it is the one thing in this commit the owner may want to overrule.
- **Class:** a starter's figures come from two samples (the API line, the Statcast frame) and nothing asked whether they describe the same starter. One mechanism: `incomplete_sample` compares the regular-season plate appearances of the frame (the API's window; the postseason never fills a gap) with `battersFaced`; short is incomplete, an empty frame next to a line above 0 is the extreme case; the correction degrades what cannot be trusted (pitch data, count `null`, no scope) and keeps the rest (API counts, hand), with a recorded `statcast_pitcher` error. Separately `provenance.impossible_figures` (`rule1:impossible_figures`) holds the strict invariants. `_plate_appearance_events` is now the one definition of "events usable" for the counts and for the comparison.
- **What changed:** `live_pipeline.py` (the constant, `_plate_appearance_events`, `regular_season_plate_appearances`, `statcast_is_short`, `incomplete_sample`, the correction in `fetch_pitcher_statcast`, `_attach_sample(pitches_known=...)`, `_warn_unmapped_pitch_types` moved after the decision so a warning is not recorded for pitch data that is not used), `provenance.py` (`impossible_figures`, `arsenal.pitches` may be `null` and then no pitch of his is in the mix, `arsenal.scope` nullable), `DATA_CONTRACT.md` (rule 1 check, rule 2 table, 6.3, new 6.5, F21 done), the matrix (the fake API line is a function of the frame, `stat_line`; the hard cases `statcast_gap`, `statcast_short`, `statcast_short_season`, `unknown_figures`), `golden_scenarios.py`, the golden file.

### 2026-10-15 — Agency workflow: D35, the reviewer, the git guard (configuration, no product code) (Claude Code, gated; the last commit with an individual yes)

- **Owner's instructions:** add D35 to `DECISIONS.md` with his text; a read-only reviewer subagent with a mandatory checklist; a PreToolUse hook on Bash for the git rules, with tests; the section "Agency workflow" in `AGENTS.md`; `CLAUDE.md` is only `@AGENTS.md`. Then commit f of D2.2 under D35, and at the close of D2.2 the summary in STATUS and the plan of D2.3 in plan mode, stopping for his approval.
- **What changed:** `docs/DECISIONS.md` (D35 between D34 and D36; the "Why" column is my wording, the decision text is the owner's; D20's Status column reads "Closed; amended by D35", authorized by the owner, nothing else of D20 changed), `.claude/agents/reviewer.md`, `.claude/hooks/git_guard.py`, `.claude/settings.json` (the hook wired; the attribution setting that was there stays), `tests/test_agent_git_guard.py`, `AGENTS.md` (the section, plus one clause in "After EVERY finished task" and one in "Git rules" so that D35 and D20 do not contradict inside an approved plan), `docs/STATUS.md`. `CLAUDE.md` already was the single line `@AGENTS.md`.
- **The guard:** blocks a push to any branch but `v2` or `experiment/*`; any mutating operation that names `main`/`master` or a tag (reading them is allowed: `git diff main...HEAD`, `git tag --list`); `--force`, `--force-with-lease`, a `+refspec`, `-f` in the destructive subcommands, `rebase`, `reset --hard`, `--no-verify` (also `-n` of `commit`), an override of `core.hooksPath`; `commit --amend` when HEAD is on a remote (and when git cannot say); a commit, merge, revert, cherry-pick or am whose author or committer is not Daniel Herdenez <edherdenez@gmail.com> (from `-c`, the environment of the command, the environment of the process, `--author` or the config), and a change of that identity. The bodies of here-documents are text, not commands.
- **Tests:** 120 (43 commands of a normal day that must pass and 54 dangerous ones that must be blocked with their reason; the chain, the here-string, the current branch, amend, the identity in 10 forms, the exit codes of the script as Claude Code reads them, the wiring in the settings). Mutation checks: removing each rule turns at least one test red (the generic `--force` rule needed four more cases: `checkout --force`, `branch --force`, `clean --force`, `worktree add --force`). The first real use found a false positive, fixed with tests first: the guard read `2>&1` in `git push origin v2 2>&1 | tail -1` as a refspec and blocked the session's own push; redirections (`2>&1`, `> file`, `&>`) are now not arguments of the command (7 allowed and 3 blocked cases; removing the rule turns 3 tests red).
- **Limits, stated:** the parser reads the commands an agent writes (quotes, `&&`, `;`, `|`, heredocs), not every shell construct; a command it cannot read is let through, and the `commit-msg` hook and CI are the other lines. The reviewer's Bash restriction (git read commands and the verification commands) is an instruction of its file, not a technical limit.
- **Verification:** see the commit.

### 2026-10-15 — P0.15-D2.1c the critic's notes and the demo's derivations (F8, F18) (Claude Code, gated)

- **Owner's instructions:** first confirm the CI of `e6cc166` (green: jobs `pytest` and `components`, run 38791321135) and diagnose the impossible counts of the new `live_both_limited` golden (report below), then D2.1c from `docs/plans/P0.15-D2.1.md`: F8 with the sentence "No betting or outcome-prediction language found.", F18 correcting Demo Pitcher C with a test that recomputes the derivations of each demo matchup with the live functions; tests red first; show the golden diff and the served JSON diff before the commit.
- **Class:** a note that asserts a check without looking at it (F8, rule 4), and a derived value of the demo that disagrees with its data (F18, rule 4).
- **What changed:** `agents/critic.py` (the note is written only if the language scan returned `not_present`), `agents/messages_en.py` (`critic.note.no_betting`; `focus` and `stuff_plus` out), `claims_registry.py`, `labels.profile_pitches` (extracted from `process_pitcher_statcast`, same behavior), `demo_inputs.py` (Demo Pitcher C put-away: Curveball; title of `matchup_001` and its archive row; the stale constants out of the demo's own critic notes), `known_violations.py` (three entries out, 27 left: `critic.note.stuff_plus`, `analyst.watch.action.land#putaway_share` and, hidden by the fix of F18, `generator.workflow.checked#count` of F4, whose rule still fires in `tests/test_contract_checks.py` if the demo has a warning), `DATA_CONTRACT.md`, the golden file and the served demo file.
- **Served file diff (18 paths):** the two constant notes become the one note in both matchups; `matchup_002`: put-away pitch Curveball, the critic's check for it is supported (26%) instead of a warning (so its summary reads "found them supported" and the warning count of the workflow step is 0), the report names the curveball in its sections, watch question and short summary, and the postgame plan sentence says "curveball counts"; `matchup_001`: the title and the archive row list the away team first. The claims lists follow.
- **Golden diff:** every scenario loses the two constants for the one note (15 paths); `demo_final` (Demo Pitcher C) also changes where he is named (critic check, strengths, style read, watch question, short summary, postgame plan). No live scenario changes otherwise.
- **Seen failing first:** 17 tests (the note, the retired keys, the derivations of the demo, the contract). Eight of them failed only for the missing function `profile_pitches`; once it existed the derivation tests failed for the data (Demo Pitcher C's put-away pitch, the title and archive row of `matchup_001`, and a too strict style check of my own that I corrected: the demo's prose says "fastball" for a four-seam).
- **Diagnosis of impossible counts (owner's question, no code changed):** the golden `live_both_limited` published "40 pitches" and "400 batters faced" for one starter, and `live_limited_arsenal` already did before D2.1b. Cause in the fixtures: `sample()` of `tests/test_limited_samples.py` defaults to 400 batters faced whatever the pitches. Scanned over 98 live matchups (4 corpus kinds, 13 live golden scenarios, 72 matrix cells, 10 live hard cases) for `batters_faced > pitches`, `home_runs > batters_faced`, `K + BB (+ HR) > batters_faced` and `batters_faced < outs of the innings`: 0 in the corpus, 3 golden scenarios (`live_both_limited`, `live_limited_arsenal`, and `live_limited_rates_only` with 50 batters faced for 101 innings), 52 of 72 matrix cells (the fake API line says 400 batters faced for a starter whose frame has 0 or 60 pitches, and answers the same line on opening day) and 4 hard cases. Every one of them is published as `fetched_snapshot` with no run error and no failed check. HR > BF and K + BB + HR > BF never occur. Sources: pitches are Statcast's (the frame from opening day to the cutoff, rows with a `pitch_type`, regular season and postseason); batters faced, strikeouts, walks and home runs are the MLB API's when it gives all four (regular season only) and Statcast's otherwise; innings, ERA and WHIP are the API's. So the pipeline compares no two of them. In the real world the pair is possible only when Statcast has a gap the API does not (tracking outage or a game with no `pitch_type`, a truncated answer cut at a row boundary, a publication lag of Savant): then a starter with one start looks like a debut (0 pitches) next to an API line of 25 batters faced. Rare, but nothing would notice. Caveats for a rule: an intentional walk is a batter faced with no pitch (negligible for a starter), and a runner out on the bases can make outs exceed batters faced (so the outs check is not strict). Also: D24's thresholds make "arsenal limited and strikeout/control not limited" unreachable with consistent sources (100 pitches is about 26 batters faced, under the 70 of strikeout), which is why `live_limited_arsenal` can only be built from inconsistent figures.
- **Proposal to the owner (not decided):** a new finding F21, rule 1 (figures of one entity must be possible together), closed in P0.15-D2.2 (the same commit that makes an unusable Statcast column a recorded source error, F6a/F6b): (1) the manifest of section 6 gets an invariant per starter, `batters_faced <= pitches`, `home_runs <= batters_faced`, `strikeouts + walks + home_runs <= batters_faced`, code `rule1:impossible_figures#...`, in the table until the fix; (2) `_attach_sample` compares the two sources and, when the API counts exceed what the Statcast pitches allow, records a `statcast_pitcher` source error ("incomplete: N pitches for M batters faced") and publishes no pitch data for that starter (pitches unknown, as for a debut), keeping the API counts: the API is the official line and Statcast is the one with a possible gap; (3) the fixtures and the fake sources of the matrix are made consistent in that same commit (the 3 golden scenarios, the fake API line of the matrix, scaled to the frame, and none on opening day), because the new check would flag them. Not in D2.1c: the fixtures cannot be fixed alone without hiding that the pipeline publishes the pair.
- **Verification:** see the commit.

- **Owner's instructions:** the differential test of the critic against `data_pipeline/report_facts.py` over the corpus and the 72 cases (owner, 2026-10-13), a disagreement is a bug in one of the two and is fixed, not tolerated; if the fix changes the golden text, stop and report first (2026-10-14); report before the commit if the suite goes over about 5 minutes.

Test 1: on the 45 distinct reports of the 204 matchups of the corpus, the matrix and the hard cases, who each sentence is attributed to (`critic._owned_sentences` against `facts.owned_sentences`), the pitches each names, the pitches each starter threw, and the verdict and the evidence text of the names, limited-arsenal, command, scope and betting checks (the expected text is rendered from what `report_facts` finds, so a different pitch, owner or term is a different string). Tests 2 to 14, one per world: the same on reports with one injected sentence, in 13 matchups chosen by greedy cover so that every combination (limited arsenal, groups that differ, command label) of a starter and every relation of the two pitch sets is present. The sentences: every pitch and alias (a rotating part of them in each world, since how a pitch is named does not depend on the world), said about either starter, both or nobody, in the section of either starter, a neutral section, a bullet or the headline; command, scope (onto a bare report: a real report of a starter whose groups differ already says so) and betting phrases (where they sit matters, the critic reports the location); and awkward texts (sentences glued or split by a newline, both names in a title, a title for one starter and a body for the other, a name in lower case, a starter named after a pitch). Controls: the injected sentences reach every branch of the critic's data checks (including a below-average command and a scope claim of one sample), the worlds contain a limited arsenal, differing groups and a shared pitch, and the harness fails when one reader is made wrong.
- **Result: no disagreement.** The two readers agree on every report and every sentence tried. No change was made to the critic or to `report_facts`, so the golden text did not move.
- **Seen failing (mutations of the critic, restored after):** the critic ignores the title of the section when a sentence names nobody: the tests of the corpus and of the worlds fail; the critic stops reading `solid` as a strong command: the same. The meta test also makes `report_facts` wrong in two ways (`_named_pitches` empty, `betting_terms` empty) and requires the comparison to report it. The tests themselves were written after seeing the first run pass (nothing to fix), so the red step is the mutations.

It was reduced (worlds by greedy cover, forms rotating over the worlds) before the commit: `python -m pytest` now takes 3 minutes 52 seconds (it was 3 minutes 11 seconds); the differential costs about 40 seconds once the matrix is cached.
- **Documents:** `DATA_CONTRACT.md` (rule 4: what the differential measures), `PLAN.md` (D3.3b-3, D3.3b and D3 ticked), `STATUS.md` (next task D2.1).
- **Limits, seen and left:** the sentences are made from the vocabulary of the catalog (23 pitch forms, the betting terms, the command and scope markers), so a form that is in neither reader is in neither test; the worlds are 13 of the 204 matchups (the reports of all of them are read as they are); the differential does not read the pitch-role checks (`critic.pitch.*`), which the registry already judges on the raw counts, nor the summary. The injection layer assumes that how a pitch is named does not depend on the matchup, which is why its variants rotate over 13 matchups chosen for coverage instead of crossing with all 204; the branch-coverage test backs it, by showing that the injected sentences reach every branch of the critic's data checks in those matchups.
- Local run (default terminal, nothing changed in PATH or locale): `python -m pytest` 1939 passed, 1 skipped, 11 xfailed; `npm test` 67 passed. No TypeScript changed, so `tsc`, lint and `next build` were not run.

### 2026-10-13 — P0.15-D3.1 phrases and claims (Claude Code, gated)

- **Mechanism (a simplification of the approved design):** the plan said a context (`contextvars`) would collect the phrases while the agents run. Collecting turned out to be unnecessary: `Catalog.__call__` returns a `Phrase`, a `str` that carries its catalog key, its arguments and the starter it is about, so the record travels with the text and `attach_claims` finds it by walking the text fields of the payload. A phrase that never reaches the file is never recorded. The only context left is `Catalog.subject(slot)`, set by the agents where a sentence is about one starter. Phrases given as arguments stay phrases; `Catalog.join` and `Phrase.capitalize` build sentences out of sentences (pseudo keys `@join`, `@capitalize`); any other string operation gives a plain `str`, and a plain `str` in a text field is refused.
- **Where the sentences were plain strings (call sites changed, text unchanged):** the analyst joined phrases with f-strings and `" ".join` / `"; ".join` (`away_vs_home`, `_live_pitcher_read`, `_offense_read`, `_scope_notes`, `_join`), the writer in `_arsenal_scout` and `_confidence_note`, the critic in the unsupported-names and betting-terms evidence, and the generator wrote the workflow, the data label and the live confidence note as f-strings (now keyed templates in `data_pipeline/generator_texts.py`, text identical). The 14-scenario English golden file and the served demo text are unchanged (the regenerated `public/data/pitchcraft-demo.json` differs only by the version, `metadata.language` and the claims).
- **Files:** new `data_pipeline/claims.py`, `data_pipeline/generator_texts.py`; changed `agents/language.py`, the four text agents, `data_pipeline/{schema,validators,generate_demo_json,live_matchup,demo_inputs}.py`, `lib/types.ts` (optional `Claim`, `claims`, `language`), `DATA_CONTRACT.md` (claim record, pseudo keys), `PLAN.md` (D3 split into D3.1 to D3.3, the tolerance goes with the last fix of D2.5).
- **Tests first, seen failing:** `tests/test_phrases.py` (14), `tests/test_claims.py` (27), `tests/test_claims_payload.py` (66: four real payloads, the 14 golden scenarios, the served file). Before the fix the first two files could not even be imported (`Phrase`, `data_pipeline.claims` did not exist) and 54 of the 66 payload tests failed; the 12 that passed are controls (the validator, plain strings in the file, the generator's text unchanged).

### 2026-10-12 — P0.15-C Statcast rows validated one by one (Claude Code, gated)

- **QA4-02 and its root:** `_require_columns` checked that `game_date` and `game_type` existed, never their values; the date comparison and `isin` then read a missing value as "excluded". One function, `validate_statcast_rows(df, source)`, now runs before any filter: for a populated frame every row needs a parseable `game_date` (text or a date; a number is not a date) and a `game_type` in `R S E A F D L W`; otherwise it raises `ValueError` ("N malformed row(s) of M (...); first at row i: game_date=..., game_type=...") and the fetcher records it (`statcast_pitcher`, `savant_team_batting`). The whole answer fails, like a missing column does; I did not drop only the bad rows because the published sample would then be undercounted, which is what QA objected to. An empty frame stays valid; spring/exhibition/all-star rows and dates after the cutoff are still excluded without an error.
- **Three call sites:** `fetch_pitcher_statcast`, `derive_team_splits` and `fetch_team_batting_statcast` (each month is validated before it is trimmed and cached, so a malformed month is never cached).
- **Sweep (fixed under the rule):** in a team-batting frame the columns that decide whether a row is the team's plate appearance and against which hand (`inning_topbot`, `home_team`, `away_team`, `p_throws`) had the same hole, so `validate_team_batting_rows` checks them too (`Top`/`Bot`, non-empty teams, `L`/`R`). Checked offline against the real Savant rows already in `data_cache/raw/team_batting`: 17 files, 53,941 rows, no missing value in any of the six columns, game types `R`, and the rule rejects none of them. Seen and left: `process_pitcher_statcast` drops pitches with no `pitch_type` before counting (an unclassified pitch is not part of the mix; real Statcast has such rows) and rows with no `stand` count in the overall share only; neither is a malformed integrity column. A month already cached by an earlier run would be validated again at `derive_team_splits` and fail each run until its file is deleted; `data_cache` is git-ignored and CI starts empty.
- **Tests first, seen failing:** `tests/test_statcast_rows.py`, 36 cases: nine malformed pitcher shapes (type missing in every row / in one row, unknown, lower case, empty; date missing in one row / in every row, not a date, a number), the two QA probes (one missing date among 170 rows; a type missing everywhere through `run_live_pipeline`, which ended `ok` with 0 pitches and now ends `failed` with the error), eleven malformed team shapes (type, date, half-inning, teams, hand), a malformed month that is not cached, and the controls (empty frame, `S` `E` `A` rows, later dates, `F` `D` `L` `W` rows, dates as text, a valid team split). 23 of 36 failed before the fix (17 first, then 6 for the team columns).

### 2026-10-11 — P0.15-B one scope per group of figures (Claude Code, gated)

- **QA4-01 and its root:** `_attach_sample` copied the MLB API's ERA, WHIP and innings and the fallback counts into one `season_summary` with one `scope` (the counts' scope). With the API missing one count and Statcast including the postseason, the regular-season ERA was labelled "regular season and postseason", and the analyst, which only compared the pitch data with the counts, wrote "all pitcher figures cover the regular season and postseason". There are now three groups, each with the scope of its source, and a single function reads them: `labels.figure_scopes(starter)` returns `pitch_data` (Statcast, only if pitches were counted), `season_rates` (ERA, WHIP, innings: the API, regular season only, only if one is available) and `counts` (the source that gave them, none if unavailable). `labels.scopes_differ(starter)` and `labels.stated_scope(starter)` sit on top of it. `season_summary.scope` is gone; `season_summary.scopes = {season_rates, counts}` replaces it (null for a group with no figures); `sample.rates.scope` stays, equal to `scopes.counts`.
- **Everything that read a scope now reads it from there (sweep, fixed under the rule):** the analyst's scope note (same wording as before when the groups agree or when the API and the counts agree against the pitch data; a new sentence listing each scope with its figures when the API's ERA and the Statcast counts differ), the rate qualifier (it said "(regular-season rates)" always; it now states the scope of the counts behind the label, "(regular season and postseason rates)" for Statcast counts with postseason pitches), the critic's scope check (new: a sentence that says "all pitcher figures cover" about a starter whose groups differ is `unsupported`, even if the report also says they differ elsewhere) and its walk-rate evidence (it quoted `sample.rates.scope`; it quotes the counts' scope), the data-cutoff check of publication validation and the generator's `_starter_throughs` (they read the old single scope), and the starter card. A test fails if any module of `agents/` indexes a scope directly. Unchanged on purpose: the detail panel's "Pitch data: <scope>" (that group's own scope) and the team offense scope (a separate group).
- **Validation:** `_validate_figure_scopes` fails a live starter whose summary has no scope pair, whose ERA/WHIP/innings are available but not scoped regular season (or unavailable and scoped), whose counts scope differs from the sample's, or does not agree with the source (the API: regular season; Statcast: the same kind as the pitch data; unavailable: no scope). `_game_unsafe` rejects a snapshot without the pair (older cached snapshots, until the next run).
- **Card:** one caption ("ERA, WHIP and K: <scope>") when the groups agree; two ("ERA and WHIP: <scope>", "K: <scope>") when they differ; only the group that has a scope; nothing if none.
- **Tests first, seen failing:** `tests/test_figure_scopes.py` (33 cases: the QA probe, API complete / missing one count / only ERA-WHIP-IP / absent × regular-only / with postseason through the real fetchers, generator and `validate_payload`; the report text per case; the qualifier; six validator rejections; the critic; unit tests of the function; the guard) 27 failed, 6 passed before the fix (cases where the old behaviour was already right); 4 of 9 in `StarterCard.test.tsx`. Fixtures that wrote the old single scope (`golden_scenarios`, `test_limited_samples`, `test_live_starters_no_demo_fields`, `test_snapshot_dates`, `test_stale_snapshot`, `test_live_sample_pipeline`, `test_partial_live_snapshot`, `test_labels`, the component fixtures) were updated to the pair, not weakened; the 13 existing scenarios of the English golden and the regenerated demo JSON are unchanged.
- **Debut with no figure (owner's decision, done in this task):** a starter who has no figure at all (no pitch, no ERA/WHIP/innings, no count) used to get "<name>: all pitcher figures cover the regular season through D" in the confidence note. `_scope_note_plan` now returns "none" for him and the note reads "<name>: no season figures are available." (catalog key `analyst.scope.none`); a debut next to a starter with figures gets his own note beside the other's scope note. A BMP symbol (`✨ generated by codex`) was stripped under `C.UTF-8`; the robot, outside the Basic Multilingual Plane, was not: Git for Windows' `grep` does not match `[^[:alnum:]]*` over such a character in a UTF-8 locale (cause inferred from that contrast, not read from grep's source). Fix in `.githooks/commit-msg`: `in_c() { LC_ALL=C "$@"; }` and every `grep` and `sed` that reads the message goes through it, with the message read from stdin so the C locale never touches a file name. - **QA2-02:** `critic._owned_sentences` gives every sentence of the report the starter it is about: a sentence that names only one starter is his; one that names nobody is his if the title of its section names only him; one that names both is nobody's. `_limited_arsenal_check` reads only the sentences of that starter. - **Lint:** `.pytest_cache/**` is in the ESLint ignores; `lib/__tests__/lint-config.test.ts` asks ESLint itself whether `.pytest_cache` and `.next` are ignored and the code is not.

### 2026-10-11 — P0.12-D minimal D6: `language` parameter and a message catalog (Claude Code, gated)

An unknown language raises instead of falling back to English. The writer's and the critic's language-specific checks (forbidden and required terms, betting words, the "different samples" marker, how a text says a pitch: "two-seam", "cut fastball", …) are catalog entries too. Spanish is not written; it is a second `messages_xx.py` with the same keys.

They need the same treatment before Spanish ships (v2.1).

A pseudo-language catalog (`«key»`) proves the text comes from the catalog: no English sentence of the agents survives in the output of any of the 13 matchups (a fragment that is also in the input data does not count). A key check fails on a key used but missing, or in the catalog but unused. An AST guard fails on any string constant in `agents/*.py` that has a space and a word (docstrings and `raise` messages excluded), with a test that proves the guard catches a sentence.

### 2026-10-11 — P0.12-B the Critic blocks publication, CI validates the served JSON (Claude Code, gated)

- **QA-04:** `process_pitcher_statcast` no longer invents pitches: the put-away pitch is the second pitch only from 15% of the mix (`labels.PUTAWAY_MIN_USAGE_PCT`), otherwise "Not available"; no pitch mix means no pitch named at all (it used to say "Fastball"). The analyst and writer cope with it ("works mainly off a four-seam", a neutral what-to-watch question, style "Four-seam as the main pitch"). The Critic never emits `revised`: a profile pitch absent from the mix is `unsupported`, a low share is a `warning`, and a new check marks `unsupported` any pitch name in the report text that is not in the game's pitch mix. `_run_agents` raises `CriticBlocked` on any `unsupported` check, `main()` returns 1 and does not write the JSON, and the validator rejects `unsupported` and the removed `revised`. The workflow text counts warnings, not revisions. Consequence for P1.2: the daily job must keep the previous JSON when the generator exits 1.
- **Pitch names in the report text (owner's follow-up, D30):** the critic's name check now knows aliases (`two-seam`/`sinker`, `cut fastball`/`cutter`, `change-up`, `split-finger`, `curve ball`, `4-seam`; plain `fastball` needs any fastball-family pitch) and holds a sentence that names exactly one starter to that starter's pitches; a sentence naming both or neither is held to the two together (the limit of reading free text; a pronoun sentence is not attributed). Before: aliases were not scanned at all (a fabricated "two-seam" or "cut fastball" passed) and the scope was the union of both starters (10 of 28 tests in `tests/test_critic_pitch_names.py` failed); no false positive on correct alias text. D30 records the 15% put-away rule as a simplification to review when pitch data by count exists.
- **"revised" in docs:** only `docs/KAGGLE_WRITEUP.md` (the archived v1 submission, kept unchanged by its own header) describes the old REVISED chip; `PRD_v2.md`, `eval-spec.md` and `PLAN.md` say nothing that conflicts with the new schema, so nothing else changed.
- **QA-01:** `tests/test_served_json.py` checks the file `lib/data.ts` serves (validator, mode honesty, no demo field on live matchups, no `revised`/`unsupported`, no `N/A` in report text, pitch names in the mix); invariants, not a comparison with a regeneration, so a live file from the bot passes too. The file was regenerated with the corrected generator from an empty cache (command in Current state): `demo_cached`, demo names, no live claim.
- **"N/A is never a number" (owner's addition):** `tests/test_na_is_not_a_number.py` — no agent output names or computes ERA, WHIP or IP when they are `N/A` or `None`; the analyst's scope note lists only the figures that exist (before it named ERA/WHIP/innings regardless); both spellings give identical output. The frontend half (the card shows "—") needs the component runner, so it is a test in P0.12-C; the card still shows the string "N/A" until then.
- Tests first and seen failing: 18 of the 30 new tests failed on the old code (the rest are guards or live-file-only). Older fixtures that named pitches missing from their own chart (`test_limited_samples.py`) now include them in the chart. Local run: 296 passed, 1 skipped (a live-file-only test); `tsc`, lint and `next build` clean.
- Next: P0.12-C.

### 2026-10-11 — P0.12-A strict input boundary (Claude Code, gated)

- **QA-02 (D29):** `resolve_as_of(game_date, as_of)` in `live_pipeline.py`: the cutoff is the day before the game's official date (`officialDate` from the schedule, New York), never the runner's UTC clock. `fetch_todays_games` returns `official_date`; its default date is today in New York. `build_live_snapshot` resolves the cutoff per game and passes it to every pitcher and team fetch; an explicit `as_of` on or after the game day is rejected (the run is recorded as failed). The snapshot and the run record carry the resolved `as_of`. The Savant budget for the month that holds `as_of` is separate (10 requests per run, `SAVANT_MAX_OPEN_MONTH_REQUESTS_PER_RUN`); when spent, that team's splits are unavailable with an error. Both Savant counters now reset at the start of each run. Deleted `test_build_live_snapshot_without_as_of_keeps_todays_behavior`; the e2e leak control now calls the fetcher with a late cutoff instead of the pipeline default. The fetchers keep `as_of=None` meaning today (the pipeline is the boundary).
- **QA-05:** pitcher Statcast needs `game_date` and `game_type` on a non-empty frame, and a `None` result is an error; `derive_team_splits` needs its seven columns (the `game_type` filter is no longer optional). An empty frame (a debut) is still valid. A schedule without a `dates` list is an error, so the run is `failed`, not `no_games`.
- **QA-06:** `_snapshot_mismatch` in the generator: a snapshot is live only with the same non-empty `run_at`, the same game date and the same `as_of` as the run. Two missing identifiers are no longer "equal".
- **QA-08:** `_apply_live_starter` builds the summary and profile from unavailable defaults (`N/A` for ERA/WHIP/IP, `None` for counts, "Not available" pitches) plus what the snapshot supplies.
- Tests first and seen failing: `test_as_of_boundary.py` (10 of 14 failed), `test_source_structure.py` (11 of 19), `test_stale_snapshot.py` (13 of 16), `test_partial_live_snapshot.py` (5 of 6); the rest are guards that must keep passing (valid empty frame and schedule, earlier explicit `as_of`). Older stubs that the new signature or the mandatory `game_type` broke were updated, not weakened. Local run: 239 passed.
- Next: P0.12-B.

### 2026-10-10 — P0.11 close the Phase 0 gaps before the QA gate (Claude Code, gated)

- **Scenario 1 is now automated end to end** (`tests/test_as_of_end_to_end.py`): `run_live_pipeline` runs with `as_of=2026-07-14` for a game on 2026-07-15 over sources that do leak: Statcast returns the game-day pitches, later pitches and spring-training pitches regardless of the range; the season-stats API answers with obviously "future" numbers for any end date after the cutoff; the Savant team-batting source ignores the requested end date and returns 50 home runs from 2026-07-20. The test inspects the snapshot (150 pitches, the cutoff numbers, 20 plate appearances and a .000 OPS in the team splits, no leaked token anywhere in the serialized data) and the requests (every season-stats call ends on the cutoff and no Savant call goes past it). A control test runs the same sources without `as_of` and shows the leaks are really reachable. Seven deliberate breaks (Statcast date filter, spring training allowed, season stats ignoring the cutoff, team-split date filter, Savant asked past the cutoff, `as_of` dropped for pitchers, `as_of` dropped for team splits) are all caught. Local run: 185 passed.
- **D28** splits scenarios 4 and 9 so each part is verified where it is built; D4 is marked "amended by D28". Phase 0 keeps: the pipeline exits successfully without errors and records `no_games` (4), and the series-status fetch returns the standing before first pitch for a given `as_of` (9). Moved: "the site shows the season archive" is now an acceptance criterion of P1.6; "the historical dataset builds every postseason case this way" is an acceptance criterion of P2.4. Nothing was dropped.
- **Gaps assigned to tasks:** component tests for the explicit "not available" states go to P1.4 (the repo has no test runner for components); pinning pandas and numpy goes to P1.0; counting the pitcher Statcast fetch in a request budget goes to P1.2 (D23's 210 covers only the team-batting fetch, so a budget for pitcher requests needs its own figure, proposed as a new decision).
- **Accepted risk (owner, 2026-10-10):** `npm audit` including dev dependencies shows 5 high findings, one chain under `eslint-config-next` (`@next/eslint-plugin-next` → `fast-glob` → `micromatch` → `braces`, a stack-exhaustion denial of service in glob matching). They are development tooling, never shipped, and `npm audit --omit=dev` reports 0. npm's suggested fix is a downgrade to `eslint-config-next@14.2.35`, which is not taken. Recheck when `eslint-config-next` publishes a newer chain.
- Next: P0.10, the QA gate (the owner starts Codex).

### 2026-10-10 — Phase 0 definition-of-done check (Claude Code, self-check before the QA gate)

Method: each Gherkin scenario of `PLAN.md` mapped to the automated tests that cover it, then checked that those tests can fail: 13 deliberate breaks of the code (date filters removed, the old name split restored, a threshold moved by one, a failed run treated as live, the debut starter dropped, demo bio inherited again, …), each run against the tests that should notice it, with the file restored afterwards. First pass: 12 caught, 1 survived. The survivor was a weak test (`test_limited_arsenal_makes_no_arsenal_claim_beyond_the_limitation` compared pitch names case-sensitively while the analyst writes them in lower case, so a report that described a limited arsenal still passed). Fixed in the same commit; second pass: 13 of 13 caught. Also green: `pytest` 183/183, `tsc --noEmit`, eslint, `next build`, `npm audit --omit=dev` (0), no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`.

| # | Scenario | Evidence | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | As-of cutoff prevents leakage | `test_fetchers_as_of.py`, `test_team_splits_as_of.py`, `test_season_not_hardcoded.py` (59 tests): Statcast, season stats and team splits stop at `as_of`; the pipeline passes it to every fetcher | **Partial.** No single test runs the pipeline with `as_of` and inspects the resulting snapshot; series status is not part of the packet yet (P1.5) |
| 2 | Pitcher name is never split | `test_report_writer_names.py` (24) | Covered for the rules writer |
| 3 | Limited sample is flagged, not filled | `test_labels.py`, `test_limited_samples.py` (49) | Covered |
| 4 | No games scheduled | `test_data_mode.py`: exits 0, no errors, status `no_games` | **Partial.** "The site shows the season archive" is P1.6, not built; today the site would show demo content labelled `demo_fallback` |
| 5 | Data source failure is explicit | `test_data_mode.py` (20), including the Statcast-down run end to end into the generator | Covered |
| 6 | Debut with zero pitches | `test_live_sample_pipeline.py`, `test_limited_samples.py` (7 selected); checked live with a pitcher who has no 2026 pitches | Covered |
| 7 | Live starters carry no demo fields | `test_live_starters_no_demo_fields.py` plus broadcast note and strength profile tests (22 selected) | Covered |
| 8 | Labels use rates, not counts | `test_labels.py` (same rate, different innings, same label) | Covered |
| 9 | Series status has no leakage | `test_fetchers_as_of.py` series tests (6): the standing is the one after the last earlier game, later games never appear | **Partial.** Fetcher only; the "historical dataset" that would call it is P2.4 |

Known gaps to expect from the QA gate (P0.10), none fixed here:
- Scenarios 4 and 9 describe behavior that belongs to Phase 1 and Phase 2 but sit in the Phase 0 definition of done. Decision for the owner: move them to those phases' criteria, or accept Phase 0 closing with them partial. I did not edit the Gherkin.
- Scenario 1 would be fully automated by one more test: run `run_live_pipeline` with `as_of=2026-07-14` over mocked sources that include later rows and assert the snapshot has none of them. Cheap; not written yet.
- Frontend states ("Not available", "—", empty strength profile) are checked by `tsc` and `next build` only; there is no component test setup.
- Python dependencies other than `pybaseball` are not pinned (pandas and numpy come in transitively).
- The pitcher Statcast request (`statcast_pitcher`) is outside the D23 request counter, which covers only the team-batting fetch.
- Five dev-only high findings in `npm audit` under the `eslint-config-next` chain (documented in the P0.9 entry).

### 2026-10-10 — D25, D26, D27, P1.10 and P0.10 (Claude Code, docs only)

- **D25:** the rate bands used by P0.5 (strikeout 25% / 18%, walk 7% / 10%, rounded quartiles of 243 starters with 5+ starts) are now a decision, so a change to them goes through `DECISIONS.md`.
- **D26:** Vercel Web Analytics on Hobby, page views only; measurable things get their own route (`/quality` now, per-game routes later); active from the merge of v2.0-a, not before. Limits checked against Vercel's documentation: 50,000 events a month included and shared across the account (so with the personal site), no custom events on Hobby, one-month reporting window, a 3-day grace period then paused collection past the limit, no extra purchase. Two wording points from the docs: they say no *third-party* cookies (visitors are identified by a request hash discarded after 24 hours), and collection exists only after Enable is clicked in the dashboard. The P1.9 attribution and disclaimer text should not promise more than that.

P0.10 `[gated]` is the gate for Phase 0, after the definition-of-done check below.

### 2026-10-10 — P0.5 rate-based labels, limited samples, scopes (Claude Code, gated)

- **One module, `data_pipeline/labels.py`:** D24 thresholds (arsenal 100 pitches, strikeout 70 BF, control 170 BF), rate bands, the sample structure and the data scopes. A dimension below its threshold, or with a missing number, is `limited` and gets no label (a missing number is never read as zero). Labels come from rates only (K/BF, BB/BF): the same rate gets the same label whatever the innings. Contact risk has no rate label; the report gives the home-run count with batters faced. Removed the count-based `derive_*` / `update_pitching_profile_labels` (live pipeline) and `_derive_*` / `_sanitize_pitching_profile` (generator), and with them the `total_pitches` bug (it summed usage percentages).
- **Rate bands (my proposal, approved with the plan; they are not part of D24):** rounded quartiles of 2026 starters with 5+ starts (n = 243). Strikeout: above-average at 25% or more, below-average under 18%. Control: solid at 7% or less, below-average at 10% or more. Documented in `docs/thresholds-proposal.md`. Revisit when the season changes.
- **Scopes (owner's requirement during the task):** every group of figures carries `{kind, through, label}`. `season_summary.scope` and `sample.rates.scope`: the MLB API's ERA, WHIP, innings, batters faced and counts are **regular season only**; `sample.arsenal.scope` (pitch mix, velocity, movement): Statcast, regular season plus postseason up to `as_of` (kind is `regular_season` when no postseason pitch is in the frame); `offense_profile.scope`: regular season. If the API is down the rates fall back to the Statcast frame and take its scope. When the scopes differ the report says so ("They are different samples"), qualifies rate claims as regular-season rates, and the critic has a new check that marks `unsupported` a report that hides it. Verified live on Chris Sale as of 2026-10-08: 2,785 pitches, scope regular season and postseason; 648 BF, 200 K, 31 BB, ERA 2.16 and WHIP 0.98, scope regular season; both labels computed from the regular-season rates.
- **ERA/WHIP and postseason (the question asked):** the API's `stats=season` and `stats=byDateRange` return regular season only even when the range ends after postseason starts (Sale: 12.2 postseason IP, still 162.2 IP). Postseason needs an explicit `gameType`. Written into `fetch_pitcher_season_stats`.
- **Debut with zero pitches:** an empty Statcast frame no longer drops the game. The starter is built with no pitch mix, `pitches = 0`, every dimension limited, the throwing hand from `people/{id}` (a failed lookup records `mlb_person` and skips the game), unknown counts as `None` (the card shows "—"). Checked live with Clayton Kershaw (no 2026 pitches): built, left-handed, all limited. That run also found a false error: the API answers `"stats": []` for a pitcher without stats and the fetcher recorded it as a failure; fixed and tested.
- **Report behavior:** a limited arsenal gets one sentence ("40 pitches; 100 needed, so this report makes no arsenal claim for him") and nothing about his pitches, style or put-away pitch; the headline uses names instead of style labels; the Arsenal Scout section draws no contrast; strikeout and control claims are made only when their samples clear the threshold; the "what to watch" question becomes neutral. Demo starters (no `sample`) are untouched. The critic checks the report text against a limited arsenal and no longer crashes on a `None` label.
- **Demo leak closed:** live matchups kept the demo template's 0–100 `strength_profile` under real pitcher names (and the critic cited it as evidence). Live matchups now get an empty profile; the chart shows "Not available for live data yet".
- **Frontend:** `lib/types.ts` (`Sample`, `DataScope`, nullable labels and counts), `StarterCard` (scope caption under ERA/WHIP/K, "—" for unknown), `DetailedAnalysis` (pitch-data scope or limited note), `StrengthProfile` (empty state). Origin and limited badges are still P1.4.
- **Old snapshots** (written before samples existed) have no `sample`: every dimension is then limited rather than filled.
- Tests: `test_labels.py` (31), `test_limited_samples.py` (18), `test_live_sample_pipeline.py` (16), plus the shared fake snapshot updated to carry a sample. Written first and seen failing (labels: module missing, then one float-rounding edge fixed with cross-multiplication; limited samples: 16 of 18; pipeline: 15 of 15 at the time; the empty-stats test failed before its fix). Local run: 183 passed; `tsc --noEmit`, eslint and `next build` clean; a dry `build_payload()` passes `validate_payload` (96/96).
- Still open: "Limited sample is flagged" and "Debut" are covered at the packet and report level for the rules writer; the LLM writer (P2.2) needs the same checks. The year-over-year drift of the rate bands is not handled.

| No games scheduled | Run exits 0 with no errors and records `no_games` (P0.8). "Site shows the season archive" is P1.6. |
| Data source failure is explicit | Covered (P0.8). |
| Debut with zero pitches | **Open.** A starter with no pitches makes the game be skipped (no crash), but there is no per-dimension flag yet; it falls under P0.5. |
| Live starters carry no demo fields | Covered (P0.1, P0.1b, and the broadcast note in P0.8). |
| Labels use rates, not counts | **Open: P0.5.** |
| Series status has no leakage | Fetcher covered (P0.2); it is not part of the packet until P1.5. |

- Auto-lane run finished: P0.6, P0.7, P0.8 and P0.9, one commit each, CI green after each. Stopped here as instructed: the next items are P0.4 (needs your approval), P0.5 (gated) and P1.0 (unlabelled, so gated, and it touches `.github/workflows/`).

### 2026-10-10 — P0.8 explicit data mode and error log (Claude Code, auto lane)

- What was silent: `_load_live_snapshot` swallowed exceptions and published demo; a failed `live_pipeline` run left no record, so an old `live_matchup.json` was published as "Live snapshot fetched <old date>"; `data_mode` could only be `demo_cached` or `fetched_snapshot`; a note promised "falls back to cached demo data"; live matchups kept the demo `broadcast_note`.
- Live run record: every run of `live_pipeline.py` now writes `data_cache/processed/live_run.json` (`status`, `run_at`, `target_date`, `as_of`, game counts, `skipped_games`, `errors`). Failures are collected by `_record_error` in each fetcher (`mlb_schedule`, `mlb_season_stats`, `mlb_team_splits`, `savant_team_batting`, `mlb_series_status`, `statcast_pitcher`, `pipeline`) instead of only being printed. Status: `ok` (at least one live game; errors, if any, are listed), `failed` (nothing usable and an error), `no_games` (nothing scheduled, no error; exit 0, as the Gherkin requires), `no_usable_games` (scheduled, but none had both probables and pitch data; exit 0). Exit code 1 only for `failed`. The snapshot carries the same `run_at`.
- Generator: `_resolve_live_input` decides the mode. Live data is used only when the latest run is `ok` and its snapshot has the same `run_at`. Modes in `metadata.data_mode`: `fetched_snapshot` (live), `demo_cached` (no live run), new `demo_fallback` (a run was attempted and failed, found no games, or its record/snapshot is unreadable or mismatched). `metadata.data_status` carries mode, reason and the run summary with errors (capped at 20, messages cut to 300 characters); the fallback label reads "Live data unavailable; showing demo data". `build_payload()` was split out of `main()` so it can be tested without writing files. `config.DATA_MODE_FALLBACK` added; `lib/types.ts` has `DataStatus` and `broadcast_note: string | null`.
- Live matchups no longer inherit the demo `broadcast_note` (now `None`) and the "falls back to demo" note is gone. This closes the open finding about `broadcast_note`.
- Local state change: running the pipeline once for 2026-12-25 (no games) wrote a `live_run.json` with `no_games`, so the next local generator run reports `demo_fallback` until a live run succeeds. That is the intended behavior; `data_cache/` is git-ignored.
- Not done here (belongs to later tasks): showing the mode in the UI (P1.4 badges, P1.6 offseason state / archive for no games), and the daily job reading the record (P1.2).
- Tests: `tests/test_data_mode.py` (20 tests: error recording per source, run status table, the Gherkin "Statcast down" and "no games" end to end with a redirected cache, stale-snapshot protection, mode resolution, fallback payload validates). 9 seen failing first. Local run: 118 passed; `tsc --noEmit`, eslint on `lib/types.ts`, `npm run build` clean; a dry `build_payload` passes `validate_payload` (94/94).
- Gherkin "Data source failure is explicit" is covered by automated tests; "No games scheduled": exit and record covered, "site shows the season archive" is P1.6.

### 2026-10-10 — P0.7 no hardcoded season (Claude Code, auto lane)

- Removed `season=2026` (three fetchers) and `season_start="2026-03-20"` (two): they now default to `None`. The season is the year of `as_of` (today when `as_of` is None); its regular-season dates come from `season_dates(season)`, which reads `/api/v1/seasons/{year}?sportId=1` (2026: 03-25 to 09-27; 2025: 03-18 to 09-28), is cached for the run, and raises on failure without being cached, so callers report the data as unavailable instead of guessing. An explicit `season_start` is still honored. The remaining 2026 strings are demo data (`demo_inputs.py`) and a docstring example.
- Pitcher Statcast starts on opening day and returns None (logged) when `as_of` is before it. Team splits end at the last day of the regular season even for an October `as_of`, which keeps a team-season at 7 monthly requests (D23); checked live: NYY with `as_of=2026-10-05` gives the full-season 1963 / 4034 PA, .733 / .714 OPS, with 2 new requests (the other months were cached).
- `build_live_snapshot(target_date, as_of=None)` and the CLI (`live_pipeline.py [target_date] [as_of]`) now pass `as_of` to every pitcher and team fetch and record it in the snapshot (`as_of`, None by default). I did not derive `as_of` from `target_date` on my own: that would move daily live runs to the Savant path and refetch the current month each day, against D23's "once". The historical case builder (P2.4) passes it explicitly. This corrects my earlier note that wiring happened in P0.7 "and P2.4": the pipeline accepts it now, the callers that need it come later.
- Tests: `tests/test_season_not_hardcoded.py` (24 tests, with `season_dates` stubbed in the older fetcher tests so nothing hits the network). Local run: 98 passed.
- No change to the default live behavior; nothing to add to Pending Notion sync.

### 2026-10-10 — P0.6 starter names never split (Claude Code, auto lane)

- Root cause: `_watch_observation` in `agents/report_writer.py` split the subject of "Can <name> <action>?" at the first space (or at the third word for names starting with "Pitcher" / "Demo"), so "Can Corbin Burnes land the slider…" became "whether Corbin can Burnes land…" and even "Pitcher A" gave "whether Pitcher A land can the slider".
- Fix: the subject is matched against the full starter names of the packet (longest first); where a name ends is never guessed. An unmatched subject is left as written.
- Tests: `tests/test_report_writer_names.py` (24 cases: 11 name shapes × 2 checks, home starter, unmatched subject). 21 seen failing on the old code, then passing. Local run: 80 passed.
- Gherkin "Pitcher name is never split": covered for the rules writer by these tests. The LLM writer will need the same check in P2.2 (claims are checked against starter names).
- No product behavior change beyond the sentence; nothing to add to Pending Notion sync.

### 2026-10-10 — P0.4 threshold analysis (Claude Code, analysis only)

- Wrote `docs/thresholds-proposal.md`: sources, method, the 2026 distribution of the sample before each of the 4,858 starts, and for each dimension how many starts and pitchers each threshold would flag. Nothing implemented; `DECISIONS.md` untouched; P0.4 left unticked because its acceptance needs the owner's approval and a recorded decision.
- Measured with 27 weekly league-wide requests to the MLB Stats API (no Statcast bulk download). Start count equals the sum of final `gamesStarted`.
- Headline: strikeout at 70 BF flags 20.5% of starts; control at 170 BF flags 41.2%; arsenal at 100 pitches flags 10.7%. Home-run rate needs 1,320 BF and no 2026 starter passed 894, so it cannot be labelled honestly in one season. The 100-pitch arsenal value has no published stabilization source (closest verified: Stuff+ at 72).
- Open owner decisions are listed at the top of the document.

### 2026-10-10 — P0.3b pitcher game_type filter and Savant request budget (Claude Code)

- `fetch_pitcher_statcast` keeps only `R` and postseason (`F`, `D`, `L`, `W`) rows; spring training (`S`), exhibition (`E`) and all-star (`A`) never count, even when dated before `as_of`. Postseason rows count only when dated on or before `as_of`, through the existing date filter. This closes the open finding from P0.3. If the frame has no `game_type` column the filter is skipped.
- Savant team-batting fetch: `_savant_get` sleeps 2 s before every request except the first of the run, counts only real HTTP requests (cache hits are free) and raises `SavantRequestCapExceeded` before request 211 (30 teams x 7 monthly chunks). `fetch_team_splits` re-raises it instead of returning `{}`, so the run stops. The pybaseball pitcher fetch is a different path and is not counted.
- Tests: 7 new (1 in `test_fetchers_as_of.py`, 6 in `test_team_splits_as_of.py`; the new ones seen failing first). Local run: 56 passed.
- D23 (the 210-request ceiling) was approved by the owner and added to `DECISIONS.md` in this commit.
- Not checked, noted for P0.5 / P2.4: pitcher ERA / WHIP / IP come from the MLB API (`stats=season` / `byDateRange`); whether they include postseason games is unknown, so for a postseason case they could differ from the Statcast counts, which now include postseason games before `as_of`.
- No product behavior change; nothing to add to Pending Notion sync.

### 2026-10-10 — P0.3 team splits by date range (Claude Code, auto lane)

- **Finding:** the MLB Stats API cannot give a date-bounded split by pitcher hand. `statSplits&sitCodes=vl,vr` ignores `startDate`/`endDate` (same PA 1963 / 4034 for a window ending 2026-04-05 as for the full season); `byDateRange` honors the dates but returns one total row and ignores `sitCodes` / `opposingPlayerHand`; `byDateRangeAdvanced` and `/stats` with `sitCodes` return nothing.
- **Derived from Statcast instead.** Deviation from the approved plan: `pybaseball.statcast(team=...)` filters by *pitching* team (`player_type=pitcher` is hardcoded in the library), so it returns no batting rows for the team, and it needs one request per day (205 requests, 5.5 min per team). `fetch_team_batting_statcast` asks Savant directly with `player_type=batter&hfGT=R`, one request per calendar month (7 per team-season, never league-wide), sequential, with finished months cached trimmed under `data_cache/raw/team_batting/` (git-ignored). A month that reaches Savant's 25,000-row cap raises instead of truncating silently. `derive_team_splits` counts completed regular-season PAs by the batting team on or before `as_of`; `fetch_team_splits(..., as_of)` uses both (Savant's code for ARI is `AZ`). Without `as_of` the API path is unchanged. This replaces the `{}` that P0.2 returned.
- **Validation (the plan's stop condition), as_of 2026-10-10 vs the API's season splits:** NYY and ARI, both hands, exact match on PA, K, BB, AVG and OPS (e.g. NYY vs RHP: 4034 PA, .714 OPS both). Mid-season check, NYY as_of 2026-07-14: last game date in the data 2026-07-12, 1235 / 2367 PA vs 1963 / 4034 for the full season.
- Tests: 17 new in `tests/test_team_splits_as_of.py` (Savant mocked, cache in tmp_path; 13 failing and 4 erroring first); the P0.2 test that asserted `{}` was removed because the behavior it checked is replaced. Local run: 49 passed.
- Volume (D16): 7 sequential, cached requests per team. The 40-case eval at P2.4 is at most 80 team-seasons; each team-season is cached once, and the same cached months serve any `as_of`.
- No product behavior change (callers still use `as_of=None`); nothing to add to Pending Notion sync.

### 2026-10-10 — P0.2 `as_of` in every fetcher (Claude Code)

- `as_of` is an ISO date, inclusive (data dated on or before it); `None` = today, so current behavior is unchanged. Fetchers in `live_pipeline.py`: `fetch_pitcher_statcast` (end date = `as_of`, plus a `game_date <= as_of` filter), `fetch_pitcher_season_stats` (`byDateRange` Jan 1 → `as_of`; `as_of` must be in `season`), `fetch_team_splits`, and new `fetch_series_status`. Callers (`build_live_snapshot`) are not wired yet; that comes with P0.7 and the eval dataset.
- **Team splits (input for P0.3):** `statSplits&sitCodes=vl,vr` ignores `startDate`/`endDate` (same PA 1963 / 4034 with or without a range ending 2026-06-30), and `byDateRange` drops the vl/vr split. With `as_of` set, `fetch_team_splits` returned `{}` (unavailable) instead of leaking season-to-date numbers; P0.3 replaced that with splits derived from Statcast.
- **Series status:** the schedule's `seriesStatus` is the standing *after each game*, not the final result (checked on the 2026 ALDS/NLDS: game 3 shows "leads 2-1" while the series ended 3-1). This corrects the planning note above ("past series status shows the final result"); that only holds when reading the series' last game. `fetch_series_status` returns the standing after the last earlier game of the same series dated ≤ `as_of` (0-0 for game 1); `{}` outside the postseason.
- Tests: 18 new in `tests/test_fetchers_as_of.py` (network and Statcast mocked; 15 seen failing first, 3 were unchanged-behavior checks). Local run: 33 passed. Real calls with `as_of`: Burnes season stats before his first start → `{}`, through 2026-09-30 → 10.1 IP; Statcast through 2026-09-14 → 7.2 IP (his two starts, 3.0 + 4.2); series standings before games 1, 3 and 4 as expected.
- `requirements-dev.txt` now includes `requirements.txt`: CI only installed pytest and `live_pipeline` imports pybaseball/numpy/pandas.
- Gherkin "As-of cutoff prevents leakage" and "Series status has no leakage": fetcher level covered by the tests above; the end-to-end scenario needs the callers wired (P0.7, P2.4).
- No product behavior change; nothing to add to Pending Notion sync.

### 2026-10-10 — P0.1b live team fields without demo values (Claude Code)

- Root cause: `_apply_offense_split` returned early when a team had no split, leaving the demo template's offense fields (and `record`) on a real team; the analyst then wrote them into the report as facts. Second bug in the same path: a missing field inside a real split became the string `"N/A"`, which the analyst parsed as 0.0 and turned into "has struggled against RHP pitching (N/A OPS)".
- Fix: unavailable is `None`. `_apply_offense_split` resets `summary_label`, `ops_vs_hand`, `k_rate_vs_hand`, `bb_rate_vs_hand`, `recent_form` to `None` and fills only what the split has; team `record` is `None` for live games; `_format_ops` / `_format_pct` return `None`. `matchup_analyst` uses `_optional_rate` (no OPS claim without OPS; K% / BB% notes and pressure points only for real values; removed the unused `_rate_value`). `report_writer._confidence_note` no longer claims offense splits when a team has none. `ScoutingReport` shows "—" and "Offense data not available". `lib/types.ts` allows `null`.
- Tests: 5 new in `tests/test_live_starters_no_demo_fields.py` (no splits, partial split, agents without splits, one missing metric, demo regression). Written first, 4 seen failing on the old code (one showed the "N/A OPS" sentence), then passing. Local run: 15 passed. `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint` on changed files and `npm run build` clean. A split-less live payload also passes `validate_payload` (94 checks) in memory.
- Gherkin "Live starters carry no demo fields": covered for starters (P0.1) and team fields (this task) by automated tests.
